PQC Today capability coverage matrix

PQC Today executes selected public reference vectors, standards tests, conformance cases, and implementation probes. A passing result is evidence only for the identified test, operation, parameters, implementation build, and target. It is not an ACVTS verdict, a CAVP/CMVP certificate, or proof of exhaustive conformance.

Generated from the runtime mechanism inventory, the reviewed capability map and the registered test inventory. A status says a registered test exists for the cell; it is not a pass. Machine-readable copy: coverage-matrix.json.

Headline

  • C++: 1635 advertised capability cells (165 mechanisms). Positive: 38 covered, 748 sampled, 849 untested. Negative: 18 covered, 7 sampled, 1610 untested. 27 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 922 cells with a recorded pass, 437 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).
  • Rust: 1025 advertised capability cells (172 mechanisms). Positive: 47 covered, 470 sampled, 508 untested. Negative: 18 covered, 7 sampled, 1000 untested. 637 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 641 cells with a recorded pass, 552 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).

Rules and definitions

cell
A capability cell is one (mechanism, operation, parameter set, sign variant). Mechanism, operation (from CKF_ flags) and key-size range are DISCOVERED from the running engine (C_GetMechanismList + C_GetMechanismInfo); parameter sets, sign variants and boundaries are DECLARED in capability-map.json and kept only when the parameter set falls inside the engine-reported key-size range.
denominator
Denominator (per engine) = advertised capability cells: the engine lists the mechanism, sets the CKF_ flag for the operation, and the declared parameter set falls inside its reported key-size range. Cells the engine does not advertise, and declared capabilities no PKCS #11 mechanism can express, are counted separately as unsupported and shown — never dropped.
status
Cell status = the strongest evidence class among registered test cases that exercise the cell on that engine (order: acvts-issued > nist-reference > standard-kat > oracle > differential > round-trip > behavior-only). No registered case = untested. Not advertised = unsupported. A status says a test EXISTS; it is not a pass.
covered
Covered (per polarity) = at least 2 distinct registered cases of that polarity exercise the cell AND, for positive, at least one of them checks an externally expected value (acvts-issued, nist-reference, standard-kat or oracle). A cell is covered overall only when positive, negative, boundary and state-error are all covered.
sampled
Sampled = at least one registered case, but the covered rule is not met. One happy-path case is always "sampled", never "covered" (plan Q5).
polarity
Polarities are counted separately: a positive case never counts toward negative, boundary or state-error. Boundary = a registered case whose recorded parameters hit a declared boundary of the cell (e.g. context length 0 or 255, empty message); it may also be a positive or negative case. State-error = a case asserting a PKCS #11 state or argument error.
parity
Parity = both engines have a RECORDED PASS of the same registered case on the same artifact kind. Skipped, unsupported and not-run never count as a pass, and a recorded result counts only for the exact artifact (sha256) the mechanism inventory records. Rows with no such pair are "not established".
artifacts
Artifact kinds are reported separately: wasm (the two shipped WebAssembly engines — registered tests run in the browser), native, hardware. Native and hardware targets have not been run: they are "not run" and never counted as pass.
dimensions
Plan G-3 — two further columns per cell, from the same registered cases. Algorithm correctness = cases whose expected value is checked (acvts-issued, nist-reference, standard-kat, oracle, differential, round-trip; state-error cases excluded); covered when at least 2 distinct cases exist and one has an externally expected value. PKCS #11 API behaviour = cases that assert only PKCS #11 behaviour (behavior-only evidence: return codes, operation state, buffers, attributes) plus every state-error case; covered when at least 2 distinct cases exist and one is a state-error case. Neither column says a test passed.
inventory
Per engine and artifact kind the matrix counts, separately: advertised cells (the denominator — C_GetMechanismList × operation × parameter set × variant), registered cells (≥ 1 registered case, by evidence status), cells with a recorded pass, with a recorded fail, and with a recorded skip (the engine did not advertise a mechanism the case needs, so it was not run), unsupported cells (not advertised) and untested cells (advertised, no registered case). A skip is never a pass and is never folded into untested.
numerator
Numerators (per engine, per polarity) = advertised cells whose level is covered, sampled or untested under the rules below; covered + sampled + untested = the denominator. Per-status numerators count advertised cells by their strongest registered evidence class.

Artifact kinds and parity

  • wasm — registered; partly recorded. Registered tests target the shipped WebAssembly engines. Recorded results: 4580 from errorPathProbes (every registered error-path case, dual engine) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub b7007e6eb); 94 from katRunner (every registered KatKind) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 88609829b); 520 from useAcvpSuite (all categories, dual engine) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 88609829b). Tests without a recorded result are never reported as passed.
  • native — not-run. macOS, Linux x86-64 and Linux Arm64 native builds have not been run through these tests (plan H-2). Never counted as pass.
  • hardware — not-run. i.MX 95 and KV260 targets have not been run through these tests (plan H-3). Never counted as pass.

Rust engine accepts all 6 invalid NIST ML-KEM encapsulation/decapsulation keysopenunassignedD1-5Rust (wasm)—The NIST ACVP ML-KEM encapDecap VAL key-check cases (mlkem_encapdecap_val_test: modified H, ek values too large) are accepted by the Rust engine: its C_EncapsulateKey / C_DecapsulateKey paths perform no FIPS 203 §7.2 encapsulation-key or §7.3 decapsulation-key input checks (bundle 417c47a2; unchanged on pqctoday-hsm origin/main). C++ HashSLH-DSA wraps M′ twice — valid NIST pre-hash signatures rejectedopenunassignedD3-3C++ (wasm)—OSSLSLHDSA.cpp (bundle 7795799b, lines ~341-402) builds M′ = 0x01 ‖ len(ctx) ‖ ctx ‖ OID ‖ PH(M) itself and then passes it to OpenSSL's pure SLH-DSA via EVP_DigestSign together with OSSL_SIGNATURE_PARAM_CONTEXT_STRING, so OpenSSL wraps it again as a pure message: all 5 valid NIST pre-hash sigVer signatures are rejected and deterministic pre-hash signatures differ at byte 0. Unchanged on pqctoday-hsm origin/main. Rows stay failing on C++ (acvp.09c). Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
ML-KEM wrong-length inputs: return codes differ from PKCS #11 v3.2 and between enginesopenunassignedD1-5 / G-8C++ + Rust (wasm)—Wrong-length ciphertext at C_DecapsulateKey: Rust returns CKR_ENCRYPTED_DATA_INVALID (not in the return-value list of PKCS #11 v3.2 §5.18.9), C++ returns CKR_WRAPPED_KEY_LEN_RANGE. The C++ engine accepts a decapsulation key 1 byte short at C_CreateObject; both engines accept a short encapsulation key. The boundary rows (acvp.07b.boundary) pin each engine's observed code, so a pass there means 'behaves as pinned', not 'conforms'.
PBKDF2 iteration floor differs between engines (Rust refuses c < 1000)openunassignedG-9C++ vs Rust (wasm)—The Rust engine refuses CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 with iterations < 1000 (rust/src/ffi.rs:11125 at 417c47a2: CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, a code that does not name the parameter); the C++ engine accepts c = 1 (SoftHSM_keygen.cpp ~3012-3030 at 7795799b). A policy divergence, not an algorithm error. The c = 1 oracle cases (pbkdf2_test tests/0) are therefore not registered; katRunner and the workbench run the c = 4096 cases, which pass on both engines.
ECDSA P-521 NIST sample failed on C++ — hub CKA_EC_POINT encoding (fixed hub-side)in-progressunassignedE (ECDSA) / J-7C++ (wasm) — hub adapter—Root cause (triage 2026-09-24): hsm_importECPublicKey wrote the DER OCTET STRING length of the 133-byte P-521 point as the single byte 0x85, which DER reads as '5 length octets follow'; the C++ engine parsed a garbage CKA_EC_POINT and rejected the NIST ACVP-Server ECDSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 sample (workbench §33), while Rust passed because it reads the raw CKA_VALUE copy. Hub adapter bug, not an engine defect. Fixed by the X.690 long-form length (softhsm/helpers derOctetString); the re-recorded run shows the case passing on both engines. Kept on record until the release freeze re-captures evidence on the browser host.
SUCI key-injection helper is rejected by the C++ engineopenunassignedA-3C++ (wasm) — hub helper—hsm_injectTestKey unwraps the raw EC scalar with C_UnwrapKeyAuthenticated using a template that carries CKA_EC_PARAMS; the C++ engine rejects it with CKR_ATTRIBUTE_READ_ONLY (the same unwrap without CKA_EC_PARAMS succeeds). katRunner suci-profile-b steps 1-2 (import only, not registered) therefore error on C++; steps 3-7 import with C_CreateObject and pass on both engines. The KAT surfaces run the Rust engine. Hub helper limitation.
C++: sign/verify inits accept a key of the wrong type (CKR_OK instead of CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT)openunassignedG-8C++ (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 7795799b)—C_SignInit and C_VerifyInit with an AES secret key return CKR_OK for 33 mechanisms (CKM_ECDSA and the 9 CKM_ECDSA_SHA*, CKM_EDDSA, CKM_RSA_PKCS, CKM_RSA_X_509, CKM_RSA_PKCS_PSS and every CKM_*_RSA_PKCS / CKM_*_RSA_PKCS_PSS hash variant); C_MessageSignInit / C_MessageVerifyInit do the same for the 30 ECDSA/RSA message-sign mechanisms; C_DeriveKey(CKM_HKDF_DERIVE) accepts an EC private key. PKCS #11 v3.2 §5.13.1, §5.15.1, §5.14.1, §5.16.1, §5.18.5 list CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT and §5.1.6 defines it ("The specified key is not the correct type of key to use with the specified mechanism"). ML-DSA/SLH-DSA/HMAC/CMAC inits reject the same key correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.

Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.

Rust: Edwards / Montgomery key-pair generation accepts an inconsistent CKA_KEY_TYPEopenunassignedG-8Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2)—C_GenerateKeyPair(CKM_EC_EDWARDS_KEY_PAIR_GEN, Ed25519/Ed448) and (CKM_EC_MONTGOMERY_KEY_PAIR_GEN, X25519/X448) with CKA_KEY_TYPE = CKK_RSA in the public template return CKR_OK. §5.18.2: a template key type inconsistent with the mechanism "fails and returns the error code CKR_TEMPLATE_INCONSISTENT". Every other key-generation mechanism refuses correctly. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.
Rust: advertised cells whose operation does not executeopenunassignedG-8Rust (wasm, pqctoday-hsm 417c47a2)—The G-2/G-8 probes found advertised capability cells that fail on a valid key: CKM_ECDSA_SHA1 and CKM_ECDSA_SHA224 — C_SignInit CKR_OK then C_Sign CKR_MECHANISM_INVALID on P-256, P-384, P-521 and secp256k1; CKM_ECDSA (raw) on P-521 with a 32-byte input — C_Sign CKR_FUNCTION_FAILED (§6.3 allows any input length for raw ECDSA); AES-192 (inside the advertised 16–32 byte range) — CKM_AES_CBC_PAD C_Encrypt returns CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT and CKM_AES_GCM C_MessageEncryptInit CKR_KEY_SIZE_RANGE; CKM_EC_KEY_PAIR_GEN_W_EXTRA_BITS on secp256k1 (in the advertised 256–521 range) — CKR_CURVE_NOT_SUPPORTED. Either the operation or the advertisement (C_GetMechanismInfo range / flags, plan G-7) is wrong. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.
PKCS #11 v3.2 gives no single key-handle / key-type code for C_DecapsulateKey — not probedopenunassignedG-8C++ + Rust — specification—§5.18.9 lists CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID and CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT (not CKR_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID / CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT), while §5.1.6 says the UNWRAPPING_* codes "can only be returned by C_UnwrapKey". With no single specified value there is no key-handle or key-type probe for C_DecapsulateKey (the engines differ: C++ returns CKR_UNWRAPPING_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID for handle 0, Rust CKR_KEY_HANDLE_INVALID). Likewise C_WrapKey, C_UnwrapKey, C_DeriveKey and C_EncapsulateKey require the usage attribute but do not list CKR_KEY_FUNCTION_NOT_PERMITTED, so no key-usage probe exists for them. Specification ambiguity, not an engine defect. Recorded by the G-8 error-path probes (errorPathCatalog.ts / errorPathProbes.ts) in run-results/wasm-node-errorPathProbes.json; the rows stay failing — the assertion is the PKCS #11 v3.2 value and is not loosened.
